I stopped taking freelance art-education jobs because I couldn’t plan lessons or make myself show up. I’ve missed community events and soccer, too.\n\nEven at home, I’m not reliable with eating or showering. My friend checks on me because sometimes I’m not taking care of myself, and she worries about whether I’m safe.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Like I’m just a burden.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble organizing your daily activities or making decisions?\n\nPatient: Yes. I have a hard time keeping track of basic things now. I stopped taking freelance art-education jobs because I couldn’t plan lessons or make myself show up. I’ve missed community events and soccer, too.\n\nEven at home, I’m not reliable with eating or showering. My friend checks on me because sometimes I’m not taking care of myself, and she worries about whether I’m safe.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever seen things that you know aren't really there?\n\nPatient: I don’t really see things, no. It’s more that I hear that woman’s voice from the corner of my room. It sounds very clear, like someone is standing right next to my bed, saying I ruined everything and should stay inside.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it difficult to express your thoughts in a coherent and logical way when talking to others?\n\nPatient: Yes. I’ll try to explain what’s been going on, and my thoughts just jump around. Like I’ll start talking about the divorce papers, then suddenly I’m talking about paint colors, or the number seven, and it doesn’t connect right. People tell me they can’t follow what I’m saying, and I can tell I’m not making sense sometimes.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself unable to move or respond when you know you should, like being frozen in place?\n\nPatient: Yeah, sometimes. I don’t know if it’s exactly frozen, but my body feels like it won’t do what I want. Some days I’m just pacing from the kitchen to the window over and over until my legs hurt. Other times I move so slowly that even making coffee feels like lifting wet cement.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the disorganized way you're expressing your thoughts is a major part of what's bothering you right now?\n\nPatient: Yes, it is. It’s one of the main things upsetting me, because when I try to explain what’s happening, my mind jumps all over. I’ll start with the divorce papers, then I’m suddenly talking about paint colors or the number seven, and people keep telling me they can’t follow me. It makes me feel like I can’t get my own thoughts out right.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant changes in your sleep patterns since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Yes. I’m barely sleeping. I’ll lie awake for hours checking the door and listening for things, and then if I do fall asleep, I wake up before dawn shaking. After that I can’t really get back to sleep.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that the voice you hear is telling you the truth about you ruining everything?\n\nPatient: I don’t know. Part of me thinks it must be true when I hear it, because it sounds so real. It’s a woman’s voice from the corner of my room, saying I ruined everything and that I should stay inside. It’s as clear as if someone is standing right next to my bed.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you deserve to be punished for something you've done?\n\nPatient: Yes. I keep going over everything I did wrong, especially in my marriage and with my friends. Even small things feel like proof that I messed everything up. It’s more that I hear that woman’s voice from the corner of my room. It sounds very clear, like someone is standing right next to my bed, saying I ruined everything and should stay inside.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it difficult to express your thoughts in a coherent and logical way when talking to others?\n\nPatient: Yes. I do. I keep replaying all the things I did wrong, in my marriage and with friends, even small stuff. It makes me feel like I’m just… bad, like I hurt people by being around. I stopped taking freelance art-education jobs because I couldn’t plan lessons or make myself show up. I’ve missed community events and soccer, too.\n\nEven at home, I’m not reliable with eating or showering. My friend checks on me because sometimes I’m not taking care of myself, and she worries about whether I’m safe.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever seen things that you know aren't really there?\n\nPatient: I don’t really see things, no. It’s more that I hear that woman’s voice from the corner of my room. It sounds very clear, like someone is standing right next to my bed, saying I ruined everything and should stay inside.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it difficult to express your thoughts in a coherent and logical way when talking to others?\n\nPatient: Yes. I’ll try to explain what’s been going on, and my thoughts just jump around. Like I’ll start talking about the divorce papers, then suddenly I’m talking about paint colors, or the number seven, and it doesn’t connect right. People tell me they can’t follow what I’m saying, and I can tell I’m not making sense sometimes.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself unable to move or respond when you know you should, like being frozen in place?\n\nPatient: Yeah, sometimes. I don’t know if it’s exactly frozen, but my body feels like it won’t do what I want. Some days I’m just pacing from the kitchen to the window over and over until my legs hurt. Other times I move so slowly that even making coffee feels like lifting wet cement.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the disorganized way you're expressing your thoughts is a major part of what's bothering you right now?\n\nPatient: Yes, it is. It’s one of the main things upsetting me, because when I try to explain what’s happening, my mind jumps all over. I’ll start with the divorce papers, then I’m suddenly talking about paint colors or the number seven, and people keep telling me they can’t follow me. It makes me feel like I can’t get my own thoughts out right.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant changes in your sleep patterns since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Yes. I’m barely sleeping. I’ll lie awake for hours checking the door and listening for things, and then if I do fall asleep, I wake up before dawn shaking. After that I can’t really get back to sleep.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that the voice you hear is telling you the truth about you ruining everything?\n\nPatient: I don’t know. Part of me thinks it must be true when I hear it, because it sounds so real. It’s a woman’s voice from the corner of my room, saying I ruined everything and that I should stay inside. It’s as clear as if someone is standing right next to my bed.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you deserve to be punished for something you've done?\n\nPatient: Yes. I keep going over everything I did wrong, especially in my marriage and with my friends. Even small things feel like proof that I messed everything up. And… part of me feels like people would be safer, or at least less weighed down, if I wasn’t around.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any beliefs that you think are true, but others might think are unrealistic or untrue?\n\nPatient: Yes. I know it sounds impossible, but I’m sure the people downstairs and some of my ex’s friends are tracking when I leave. Like, the hallway lights blink right after I think about going to the store, and it feels like they know.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe you are a bad person because of your past actions?\n\nPatient: Yes. I do. I keep replaying all the things I did wrong, in my marriage and with friends, even small stuff. It makes me feel like I’m just… bad, like I hurt people by being around. Part of me believes they’d be safer or less burdened if I were gone.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're worthless because of your past mistakes?\n\nPatient: Yes. I feel worthless a lot. I keep replaying every little thing I did wrong in my marriage and with my friends, and it just feels like proof that I’m a burden. Sometimes I honestly think people would be safer, or less weighed down, if I were gone.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I do. I keep replaying all the things I did wrong, in my marriage and with friends, even small stuff. It makes me feel like I’m just… bad, like I hurt people by being around. Part of me believes they’d be safer or less burdened if I were gone.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're worthless because of your past mistakes?\n\nPatient: Yes. I feel worthless a lot. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n19\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF23, F32.3\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I’m here because I just… don’t care about anything anymore. I used to go to art classes, board-game nights, hiking, and I was thinking about getting back to soccer, but now I don’t want to do any of it. I mostly just sit in my room and let everything slip.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel sad most of the time?\n\nPatient: Yes. Most days I wake up with this heavy, empty feeling in my chest. It feels like there’s not really anything ahead of me… like my world is just the apartment walls.\n\nDoctor: Do you still enjoy things you used to like, or has the pleasure gone away?\n\nPatient: The pleasure’s pretty much gone. I used to like art class, game nights, hiking… even thought about playing soccer again. Now I just don’t care about any of it. I mostly sit in my room and let things pass.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: It became pretty constant around the following summer. So… it’s been about a year and four months now.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any strange thoughts or heard voices that others couldn't hear?\n\nPatient: Yes… I hear a woman’s voice sometimes, from the corner of my room. It says I ruined everything and that I should stay inside. It’s not like a thought in my head — it sounds clear, like someone is standing right next to my bed.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe the things the voice says are true?\n\nPatient: Sometimes I do. I know it sounds harsh, but when it says I ruined everything, it lines up with what I’m already thinking. I keep going over every mistake I made in my marriage and with my friends, and I feel like… maybe they’d be better off if I wasn’t around. Like I’m just a burden.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble organizing your daily activities or making decisions?\n\nPatient: Yes. I have a hard time keeping track of basic things now. I stopped taking freelance art-education jobs because I couldn’t plan lessons or make myself show up. I’ve missed community events and soccer, too.\n\nEven at home, I’m not reliable with eating or showering. My friend checks on me because sometimes I’m not taking care of myself, and she worries about whether I’m safe.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever seen things that you know aren't really there?\n\nPatient: I don’t really see things, no. It’s more that I hear that woman’s voice from the corner of my room. It sounds very clear, like someone is standing right next to my bed, saying I ruined everything and should stay inside.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it difficult to express your thoughts in a coherent and logical way when talking to others?\n\nPatient: Yes. I’ll try to explain what’s been going on, and my thoughts just jump around. Like I’ll start talking about the divorce papers, then suddenly I’m talking about paint colors, or the number seven, and it doesn’t connect right. People tell me they can’t follow what I’m saying, and I can tell I’m not making sense sometimes.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself unable to move or respond when you know you should, like being frozen in place?\n\nPatient: Yeah, sometimes. I don’t know if it’s exactly frozen, but my body feels like it won’t do what I want. Some days I’m just pacing from the kitchen to the window over and over until my legs hurt. Other times I move so slowly that even making coffee feels like lifting wet cement.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the disorganized way you're expressing your thoughts is a major part of what's bothering you right now?\n\nPatient: Yes, it is. It’s one of the main things upsetting me, because when I try to explain what’s happening, my mind jumps all over. I’ll start with the divorce papers, then I’m suddenly talking about paint colors or the number seven, and people keep telling me they can’t follow me. It makes me feel like I can’t get my own thoughts out right.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant changes in your sleep patterns since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Yes. It’s as clear as if someone is standing right next to my bed.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you deserve to be punished for something you've done?\n\nPatient: Yes. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I’m here because I just… don’t care about anything anymore. I used to go to art classes, board-game nights, hiking, and I was thinking about getting back to soccer, but now I don’t want to do any of it. I mostly just sit in my room and let everything slip.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel sad most of the time?\n\nPatient: Yes. Most days I wake up with this heavy, empty feeling in my chest. It feels like there’s not really anything ahead of me… like my world is just the apartment walls.\n\nDoctor: Do you still enjoy things you used to like, or has the pleasure gone away?\n\nPatient: The pleasure’s pretty much gone. I used to like art class, game nights, hiking… even thought about playing soccer again. Now I just don’t care about any of it. I mostly sit in my room and let things pass.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: It became pretty constant around the following summer. So… it’s been about a year and four months now.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any strange thoughts or heard voices that others couldn't hear?\n\nPatient: Yes… I hear a woman’s voice sometimes, from the corner of my room. It says I ruined everything and that I should stay inside. It’s not like a thought in my head — it sounds clear, like someone is standing right next to my bed.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe the things the voice says are true?\n\nPatient: Sometimes I do. I know it sounds harsh, but when it says I ruined everything, it lines up with what I’m already thinking. I keep going over every mistake I made in my marriage and with my friends, and I feel like… maybe they’d be better off if I wasn’t around. Like I’m just a burden.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble organizing your daily activities or making decisions?\n\nPatient: Yes. I have a hard time keeping track of basic things now. I stopped taking freelance art-education jobs because I couldn’t plan lessons or make myself show up. I’ve missed community events and soccer, too.\n\nEven at home, I’m not reliable with eating or showering. My friend checks on me because sometimes I’m not taking care of myself, and she worries about whether I’m safe.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever seen things that you know aren't really there?\n\nPatient: I don’t really see things, no. It’s more that I hear that woman’s voice from the corner of my room. It sounds very clear, like someone is standing right next to my bed, saying I ruined everything and should stay inside.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it difficult to express your thoughts in a coherent and logical way when talking to others?\n\nPatient: Yes. I’ll try to explain what’s been going on, and my thoughts just jump around. Like I’ll start talking about the divorce papers, then suddenly I’m talking about paint colors, or the number seven, and it doesn’t connect right. People tell me they can’t follow what I’m saying, and I can tell I’m not making sense sometimes.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself unable to move or respond when you know you should, like being frozen in place?\n\nPatient: Yeah, sometimes. I don’t know if it’s exactly frozen, but my body feels like it won’t do what I want. Some days I’m just pacing from the kitchen to the window over and over until my legs hurt. Other times I move so slowly that even making coffee feels like lifting wet cement.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the disorganized way you're expressing your thoughts is a major part of what's bothering you right now?\n\nPatient: Yes, it is. It’s one of the main things upsetting me, because when I try to explain what’s happening, my mind jumps all over. I’ll start with the divorce papers, then I’m suddenly talking about paint colors or the number seven, and people keep telling me they can’t follow me. It makes me feel like I can’t get my own thoughts out right.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced any significant changes in your sleep patterns since you started feeling this way?\n\nPatient: Yes. I’m barely sleeping. I’ll lie awake for hours checking the door and listening for things, and then if I do fall asleep, I wake up before dawn shaking. After that I can’t really get back to sleep.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that the voice you hear is telling you the truth about you ruining everything?\n\nPatient: I don’t know. Part of me thinks it must be true when I hear it, because it sounds so real. It’s a woman’s voice from the corner of my room, saying I ruined everything and that I should stay inside. It’s as clear as if someone is standing right next to my bed.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you deserve to be punished for something you've done?\n\nPatient: Yes. I keep going over everything I did wrong, especially in my marriage and with my friends. Even small things feel like proof that I messed everything up. And… part of me feels like people would be safer, or at least less weighed down, if I wasn’t around.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any beliefs that you think are true, but others might think are unrealistic or untrue?\n\nPatient: Yes. I know it sounds impossible, but I’m sure the people downstairs and some of my ex’s friends are tracking when I leave. Like, the hallway lights blink right after I think about going to the store, and it feels like they know.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe you are a bad person because of your past actions?\n\nPatient: Yes. I do. I keep replaying all the things I did wrong, in my marriage and with friends, even small stuff. It makes me feel like I’m just… bad, like I hurt people by being around. Part of me believes they’d be safer or less burdened if I were gone.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're worthless because of your past mistakes?\n\nPatient: Yes. I feel worthless a lot. I keep replaying every little thing I did wrong in my marriage and with my friends, and it just feels like proof that I’m a burden. Sometimes I honestly think people would be safer, or less weighed down, if I were gone.\n\nDoctor: Are the voices you hear a major part of your experience most of the time?\n\nPatient: Yes. It doesn’t sound like a thought—it sounds clear, like someone is standing right next to my bed.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voice and these feelings of worthlessness are something you experience almost every day?\n\nPatient: Yes… almost every day. For most of this past year, the low mood and the voice have been there together. I’ll be lying in bed feeling hopeless, and then I hear that woman telling me I ruined everything. It’s hard to separate them.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe the voice you hear is the only one that knows the truth about you and your situation?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if it’s the only one, but it feels like it knows things other people won’t say. And… I know this sounds impossible, but I’m sure the people downstairs and some of my ex’s friends are tracking when I leave. The hallway lights blink right after I think about going to the store, and it feels like that’s not a coincidence.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Part of me believes they’d be safer or less burdened if I were gone.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're worthless because of your past mistakes?\n\nPatient: Yes. I feel worthless a lot. I keep replaying every little thing I did wrong in my marriage and with my friends, and it just feels like proof that I’m a burden. Sometimes I honestly think people would be safer, or less weighed down, if I were gone.\n\nDoctor: Are the voices you hear a major part of your experience most of the time?\n\nPatient: Yes. The voice is a big part of it. I hear a woman’s voice from the corner of my room, and it tells me I ruined everything and that I should stay inside. It doesn’t sound like a thought—it sounds clear, like someone is standing right next to my bed.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voice and these feelings of worthlessness are something you experience almost every day?\n\nPatient: Yes… almost every day. For most of this past year, the low mood and the voice have been there together. I’ll be lying in bed feeling hopeless, and then I hear that woman telling me I ruined everything. It’s hard to separate them.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe the voice you hear is the only one that knows the truth about you and your situation?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if it’s the only one, but it feels like it knows things other people won’t say. And… I know this sounds impossible, but I’m sure the people downstairs and some of my ex’s friends are tracking when I leave. The hallway lights blink right after I think about going to the store, and it feels like that’s not a coincidence."
